#### Item executed this round: L.5 ProjectValidationGate — TS+lint+Rust dual-stack completion

**Why this item:** Of everything above, L.5 was the closest to "quietly wrong" — it already looked
DONE (a file existed, wired into the apply pipeline, tests were green) but was silently only
half of Law XI's mandate (typecheck only, no lint) and was **silently treating every `.rs` file
as if it had passed validation** (no check ran at all). That is exactly the kind of masked gap
Zero-MVP doctrine forbids, and it blocks *every other* Onda J/L item downstream of "AI patches a
file and something validates it" — CW6's multi-file swarm, J.2's Nexus dispatch, and any future
L.6/L.7 agentic loop all route through this exact gate.

**What was implemented (real, tested, no stub):**
- `lib/production/project-l5-lint.ts` — new: runs the project's real ESLint config (`.eslintrc.json`) in-process via the ESLint Node API (`lintText`), no subprocess (stays sandbox-safe under AgentShellPolicy #48). Fails closed on severity-2 (error) rules only, matching `next lint` defaults.
- `lib/production/project-l5-gate.ts` — new: combines `runProjectL5Typecheck` → `runProjectL5Lint` (typecheck first, short-circuits on typecheck FAIL, merges `checks[]` on PASS-through).
- `lib/production/rust-gate-unavailable.ts` — new: `isRustSourcePath` + `buildRustGateUnavailableDetail` — any `.rs` file in an AI-apply request is now explicitly **denied** with an honest "sandbox unavailable (L.1)" message instead of silently passing.
- `lib/production/project-l5-typecheck.ts` — **bug fix** (pre-existing file): the virtual `ts.CompilerHost.getSourceFile` was stubbing every unmatched `.d.ts` path (including TypeScript's own default lib files) to an empty module. Fixed to read real disk content first (including a fallback for TS's `lib.<name>.d.ts` prefix quirk when driving `ts.createProgram` directly instead of via the `tsc` CLI). Without this fix, any real-world file using `console`/`Promise`/DOM globals would have false-failed the gate.
- Wired through `agent-apply-validation-gate.ts`, `multi-file-apply-swarm.ts`, `apex-moa-provider-adapters.ts` (Auto-Heal prompt now says "Fix ALL TypeScript/ESLint errors"), `lib/server/ai-change-apply/preflight.ts`, `lib/ai/apply-preflight-user-copy.ts` (user-facing copy for `L5_LINT_FAIL` / `RUST_GATE_SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE`), `components/agents/chat/controller/useAIChatController.ts`, `lib/production/agents-merge-governance.ts` (new `denied_lint` / `denied_rust_gate_unavailable` statuses).
- Tests: `__tests__/production/project-l5-lint-gate.test.ts` (new, 9 cases) + extended `agent-apply-validation-swarm-cw6.test.ts` (+3 cases) + extended `focus-c1-apply-preflight-copy.test.ts` (+2 cases) — **all green**.
- Added `cloud-web-app/web/types/eslint.d.ts` — a narrow, scoped ambient declaration for the `eslint` package's Node API (constructor + `lintText` + `LintResult` shape actually consumed). The installed `eslint@8.57.1` ships no bundled types, and adding `@types/eslint` as a real npm dependency was tested and found to force an unrelated full dependency re-resolution across this workspace's already-fragile `node_modules` (see Environment note below) — the ambient declaration avoids touching `package.json`/`package-lock.json` entirely for a 4-method surface.

**Environment note (full disclosure, Zero-MVP honesty):** Diagnosing a stray `@types/eslint`
install side-effect surfaced a pre-existing, unrelated environment fragility: a hoisted
`@types/react@19.x` at the monorepo root was polluting the single global JSX namespace for
`cloud-web-app/web`'s `tsc` program, breaking `@react-three/fiber`'s `JSX.IntrinsicElements`
augmentation everywhere R3F tags are used (other workspace members carry their own correctly
isolated `@types/react@18.x` copies). `npm run typecheck` went from a transient **1,685-error
regression** (caused while diagnosing this) down to **91 pre-existing, unrelated errors**. **Zero
of those 91 errors are in any file this round touched or created** — confirmed by direct grep of
the typecheck output. Recorded here instead of silently omitted, per Zero-MVP: touching
`node_modules`/lockfiles in this repo is higher-risk than it looks and should be avoided in future
rounds unless unavoidable.

**Gates (Law XI dual-stack — only TS touched this round, no `.rs` files):**
- `cd cloud-web-app/web && npm run typecheck` → 91 pre-existing/unrelated errors, **0 in touched files** (verified by grep)
- `cd cloud-web-app/web && npm run lint` (`next lint`) → pre-existing errors only in untouched files, **0 in touched files**
- `npx vitest run __tests__/production/project-l5-lint-gate.test.ts __tests__/production/agent-apply-validation-swarm-cw6.test.ts __tests__/production/focus-c1-apply-preflight-copy.test.ts` → **all passed**
- Full `__tests__/production/**` regression sweep → pre-existing failures only (`nexus-squad-dispatch` missing `resolveNexusCreativeOperatorHint` — see J.2 row above — and a renderer-honesty naming drift), **neither touched this round**
- No `.rs` files touched this round → Rust gates (`cargo check`/`clippy`/`test`) not applicable per the user's own rule ("se tocar `.rs` → rust gates; se tocar TS → web gates")

#### Item executed round 2: J.2 Nexus UI — real `resolveNexusCreativeOperatorHint` + `NexusCreativeOperatorHint`

**Why this item:** explicit Founder follow-up — closes the concrete J.2 gap the P1b audit surfaced
(round 1), which was causing 4 real test-suite `TypeError`s and blocking Nexus UI from ever
rendering the correct J.5–J.10 creative-operator receipt (`AIChatActivityDeck.tsx` imports the
type but the module never exported it).

**What was implemented (real, tested, no stub):** rewrote `lib/production/nexus-squad-dispatch.ts`
end to end:
- `NexusCreativeOperatorHint` — discriminated union (`graph-operator` w/ `target: GraphOperatorTarget`, `video-to-mechanic`, `usd-integrator`, `browser-operator`, `live-voice`, `none`), matching the kinds `AIChatActivityDeck.tsx` already branches on.
- `resolveNexusCreativeOperatorHint(prompt)` — real regex keyword-routing over the mission prompt, checked in priority order (LiveVoice → BrowserOperator → VideoToMechanic → UsdIntegrator → GraphOperator sub-target keywords: sound-cue/vfx/behavior-tree/visual-script → `none` fallback for plain code missions). Not a lookup table over fixed test strings — generalizes to any prompt containing the routed phrases.
- `domainForCreativeOperator` / `successCriteriaForCreativeOperator` — map each resolved hint to a real `ApexTaskDomain` (drives Fusion model routing) and to the Law XVI / Trava success criteria a downstream Critic must uphold (e.g. browser-operator → `CostGuard settle` + `CDP farm HELD (governed fetch only)`; live-voice → `CostGuard settle` + `Duplex WebRTC HELD`).
- Fixed the real, pre-existing `dispatchNexusSquad` type drift the round-1 audit flagged: `criticalTask`/`peripheralTasks` are now genuine `ChewedWorkerTask` objects (`domain`, `successCriteria`, `generatorWidth` via `adaptiveMoAWidth(riskScore, planId)`) instead of an ad-hoc shape carrying an invalid `role` field and missing several `MaestroDelegationPlan` required properties (`missionId`, `maestroModelId`, `projectMemoryDigestId`, `lawsPackId`, `contextPackId` were silently absent before).
- `NexusSquadResult` gained `creativeOperator: NexusCreativeOperatorHint` and `recommendedMoAWidth: 1 | 2 | 3`.

**Root cause, not symptom:** did not loosen any test expectation. The 4 suites already encoded the
intended contract (`hint.kind`, `squad.creativeOperator.kind`, `successCriteria` phrases); the fix
was purely additive/corrective in the dispatch module, matching what the tests (and the already-shipped
`AIChatActivityDeck.tsx` consumer) expected all along.

|-------|--------|-------|

| `ai-v1e-j5-j7-core.test.ts` | same TypeError | **9/9 PASS** |
| `ai-v1f-j8-browser-core.test.ts` | same TypeError | **7/7 PASS** |

#### Item executed round 3: L.1 ForgeSandboxExecutor — structural sandbox blocker

**Why this item:** explicit Founder follow-up. Round 1's audit flagged L.1 as the item **8 other Onda
L contracts allegedly depend on** and as the reason `.rs` validation is fail-closed-blocked in L.5.
Re-reading `AETHEL_UNIVERSAL_IDE_FORGE_SPEC.md`'s exact contract before writing code corrected that
dependency count (see summary above — only L.2/L.4 truly gate on it; L.12/L.13 were mis-attributed),
but L.1 itself remained the genuine, spec-defined gap: no agent tool could execute *any* command
without either using a forbidden host PTY (#48) or silently doing nothing.

**What was implemented (real, tested, no stub, no `// TODO` on the shipped path):**
- `lib/production/forge-sandbox-path-guard.ts` (new) — the allow/deny primitives, independent of any provider so they're reusable and unit-testable in isolation:
  - `confinePathToProjectRoot(root, path)` — resolves both the root and the candidate through `fs.realpathSync.native` (defeats symlink escape) before a strict prefix check; denies `root_not_found` / `outside_project_root` explicitly rather than silently allowing when the root itself is missing.
  - `guardArgsWithinProjectRoot` — scans every command argument (including `--flag=value` forms) for path-shaped values and confines each one; catches `--prefix=../../etc`-style escapes hidden inside flags, not just bare `cwd`.
  - `guardCommandAllowlist` / `DEFAULT_COMMAND_ALLOWLIST` (`node, npm, npx, tsc, git, cargo, rustc, eslint`) — normalizes Windows `.exe/.cmd/.bat/.ps1` suffixes before comparison; **no shell binary (`cmd`, `powershell`, `bash`, `sh`, `pwsh`) is ever in the default list**, so even a caller that mistakenly requested one is denied by construction.
- `lib/production/forge-sandbox-executor.ts` (new) — the `ForgeSandboxSession`/create/exec/teardown contract exactly as specified (`sessionId`, `provider`, `networkPolicy`, `costGuardReservationId`, `evidenceLedgerId`, `createdAt`/`teardownAt`):
  - **`local-isolated` provider — real, DONE today, zero external dependency.** Execution goes through `child_process.execFile` only (`execFile`, never `pty.spawn`/ConPTY — structurally cannot allocate a host PTY). Every exec call re-runs the path guard on `cwd` + all args before touching the filesystem. The child environment is **built from an explicit safe-key allowlist** (`PATH`, `SystemRoot`, `TEMP`, `ComSpec`, etc.) copied from `process.env` — arbitrary host secrets (API keys, DB URLs, JWT secrets) are **never** inherited by the sandboxed process (proven by test: a fake secret env var set in the test process does not appear in the sandboxed child's `process.env`). `timeout` + `maxBuffer` are enforced natively by `execFile`.
  - **`e2b` provider — real code path, honestly env-gated.** Reuses `lib/server/e2b-runtime.ts` (Decision #52 — same module the preview runtime uses) to dynamically import `e2b` and call the real `Sandbox.create()` / `commands.run()`. This workstation has no `E2B_API_KEY` configured, so `resolveForgeSandboxAvailability('e2b')` honestly reports `e2b_api_key_missing` and `createForgeSandboxSession` denies rather than pretending to have a remote sandbox — the same honesty pattern already used by `preview-runtime-readiness.ts`.
  - **`firecracker` provider — explicit HELD, not faked.** `resolveForgeSandboxAvailability('firecracker')` always returns `firecracker_not_implemented` with the real reason (Linux KVM host required; this repo's dev/CI target is Windows). No code pretends to create a Firecracker microVM.
  - **CostGuard (Trava I) wired for real** — `createForgeSandboxSession` calls `reserveCreativeCost({domain:'forge-sandbox', estimatedTokenWeight: minutes*1000, ...})` before any provider work starts, and `teardownForgeSandboxSession` calls `settleCreativeCost` for actual elapsed minutes (refunding the estimate/actual delta) — proven by a test that reserves 3 min and settles 1, asserting the exact refunded balance. Free-tier-without-BYOK is denied before any sandbox exists (reuses the existing Trava I fail-closed rule).
  - **Evidence-backed (spec requirement, not optional)** — every session appends real `task-evidence-ledger` events: `cost` on reserve, `command` on every exec (ok/failed/**denied**, including the denial reason), `cost` again on teardown/settle. Exposed via `getForgeSandboxLedger(sessionId)` for L.5/L.6 callers and tests.
  - **AgentShellPolicy (#48) is the real arbiter, not decorative** — `createForgeSandboxSession` computes the actual provider availability *first*, then calls `evaluateAgentShellPolicy({requestedTarget:'sandbox', sandboxAvailable: <real result>})` and obeys its verdict. When a provider is unavailable (e.g. `firecracker`), the policy naturally returns `held`/`blocked` and session creation fails closed — it does **not** fall back to a host shell. Proven by test `J-ACC L1-03b`.
- **Honest, explicit limitations (documented, not hidden):**
  - `describeForgeSandboxNetworkHonesty('local-isolated')` returns `kernelLevelIsolation: false` — `networkPolicy:'none'` today only strips proxy/registry env vars on the child process; this is **not** an OS firewall / network namespace block. Real kernel-level network isolation requires a container or VM host, which is genuinely out of scope this round and is called out as **HELD** rather than claimed.
  - `firecracker` is fully HELD (no implementation attempted) — see above.
  - `e2b` remote execution is real code but **not exercised end-to-end** in this environment (no API key) — the config-gating behavior (deny when unconfigured) is what's actually tested, not a live remote exec.
  - `rust-gate-unavailable.ts` (L.5) was **not** rewired to call `execInForgeSandbox` this round — that integration (letting L.5 actually run `cargo check`/`clippy`/`test` inside a `local-isolated` session) is real, scoped follow-up work, not done here. `.rs` files remain honestly **BLOCK**ed by L.5 until that wiring lands.
  - Agent tool catalog (`agent-tool-bus-catalog.ts`) was **not** given a new `sandbox-exec` tool entry this round — L.1 is the executor primitive; wiring it into the tool-bus catalog + L.4 terminal bridge + L.6 loop is the natural next step, intentionally left for a future single-item round.

- `__tests__/production/forge-sandbox-executor-l1.test.ts` (11 tests) — provider resolution (local-isolated auto-select, firecracker HELD), network-honesty disclosure, **J-ACC L1-01** allowed command execution returns correct stdout/exitCode, **J-ACC L1-02a** command-not-allowlisted denial, **J-ACC L1-02b** `cwd` path-escape denial, **J-ACC L1-02c** path-escape hidden in an arg denial, **J-ACC L1-03** host-PTY policy still blocked, **J-ACC L1-03b** unavailable-provider session creation fails closed via the policy (not host PTY), CostGuard reserve/settle-with-refund lifecycle, free-tier-without-BYOK denial, secret-env-non-leak, unknown-session-id denial.

#### P2g — Desktop Rust kernel deep audit ([Auditoria profunda kernel Rust desktop G](1bccc11b-6558-40eb-ad06-5e81df8747cc), 2026-08-08)

**Gates:** `cargo check` (studio-local) **PASS** (~153 warnings); kernel `cargo test --lib` **613/613 PASS**; studio-local test harness **crashes on Windows** (`STATUS_ENTRYPOINT_NOT_FOUND`, known manifest issue, documented, pre-existing — not a regression). **`cargo clippy` was not run this session.**

**Architectural findings (confirms + sharpens P2c's uncommitted-WIP flag):**

1. **No unified frame/render loop exists.** `WgpuRenderer::mount_on_window` inits device/surface; present only happens through a **secondary winit probe window**, not the product Tauri WebView viewport. `GpuCullingPipeline::dispatch` is never called on any hot path (dead code, confirmed by comment in `gpu_culling.rs` itself).
2. **IPC registration regression:** `lib.rs` declares **~91** `kernel_*_wire` modules; `main.rs`'s `generate_handler!` registers only **~25** commands. Radiance, VSM, World Partition, PBD, acoustic, atmospheric probes are **compiled but unreachable from the frontend** — 143 "never used" warnings confirm this.
3. **`src/rendering/` (PBR forward, Nanite compute, bindless RT, shadow, skybox) is entirely untracked and not even compiled** — no `pub mod rendering` in `lib.rs`. Same for `src/physics/xpbd_native_compute.rs` + its WGSL, `kernel_virtual_shadow_maps_vsm_wire.rs`, `kernel_openvdb_bridge_wire.rs`. **~80+ modified/untracked/deleted files** in `apps/studio-local/src-tauri/src/` with zero commits protecting them.

**16 saltos AAA — honest completion vs UE5.5 production (not vs planning docs):** weighted average **~9–11%** (graphics pillars ~5–8%: Nanite ~5%, Lumen ~8%, VSM ~6%, OpenVDB ~3%, MaterialX ~5%; physics/audio/math probes higher at ~12–18%: XPBD ~18%, Atmospheric ~15%, Rollback netcode ~15%). Entropy GPU (bonus, not one of the 16) is the strongest at **~20%** vs Niagara — real GPU compute, 100k+ particles, soak-tested, but still not wired to the render graph. **All checked `*_aaa_ready` flags remain honestly `false`** with HELD comments — no marketing violation found in kernel flags themselves, but Progress header phrasing ("RHI Unification pure WebGPU", "Physics N=100k scale") risks overstating probe/soak work as product-ready; see header honesty note already added 2026-08-08.

**P0 risk (data loss, not feature gap):** ~80+ uncommitted `.rs` changes including 6 deleted wire files, plus the entire orphaned `rendering/` tree, sitting in the working tree with no protective commit. This is a **hygiene risk independent of the Onda G deferral** — losing this WIP (e.g. accidental `git clean`/checkout) would erase real, cargo-check-green work.

A separate **hygiene-only** pass (clippy + commit, no new wiring, no `generate_handler!` restoration) was queued to protect the WIP from loss without advancing deferred scope — see changelog.

| 2026-08-08 p2f-1 | **P2f item #1 DONE (mixed verdict) — chat/complete "CreativeBridge bypass" investigated, real gap closed** — [Auditoria profunda cloud-web-app web J/L](d8c10da3-0de0-475d-a567-7e5f7a22f434) item #1. Investigated whether `chat`/`complete`/`inline-edit`/`inline-completion`/`action`/`query` running their own metering instead of `dispatchCreativeArtifact`/`CreativeBridge` was a Law XVI Trava I security hole. **Verdict is mixed, not a single yes/no:** (a) `chat`/`chat-advanced` via `beginChatSpendSession`→`lib/ai/spend-resolver.ts` `reserveSpend`/`settleSpend` already implement a genuine two-phase reserve-before-provider-dispatch across BYOK→pool→wallet→PAYG lanes, fail-closed once all lanes are exhausted (`decideSpendLane` returns `{ok:false, lane:'blocked'}` synchronously before any provider call) — this **is** Trava I's guarantee under a different API, intentionally separate because chat has a subsidized free-tier quota (`AI_QUOTA_PRESETS.free`, 200k fast tokens/mo) rather than generative media's zero-platform-pay-on-free-tier policy; **documented, not refactored** (forcing chat onto `dispatchCreativeArtifact`, designed for one-shot artifact generation not token streaming, would be architecturally wrong — confirmed by reading `creative-artifact-bridge.ts` + `creative-cost-guard.ts` first, per the task's explicit caution). (b) `complete`/`inline-edit`/`inline-completion`/`action`/`query` used `checkAIQuota()` **alone** — a genuine, narrower TOCTOU race: it's check-then-record (`recordTokenUsage` only fires *after* the provider call resolves, fire-and-forget), so two concurrent requests for the same user both read the same "allowed" snapshot and can jointly overspend past the remaining quota before either commits. Root-caused a second contributing bug while investigating: `checkAIQuota`'s internal `usageWithPending` only folded `TokenLedger.getPendingTokens()` into the fast-pool comparison when a `modelId` was explicitly passed with weight ≤1 — all 5 vulnerable routes never pass `modelId`, so pending usage was silently dropped from the fast-pool gate entirely, not just from reservation holds. **Fix (minimal diff, reuses existing CostGuard reserve/settle *pattern*, no duplication of its pool-funding logic since chat/complete use plan-quota not wallet/BYOK pools):** added `TokenLedger.holdEstimate()`/`releaseHold()` (`lib/server/financial-ledger.ts`) — a synchronous in-memory hold map so a concurrent call observes the first call's estimate as already-pending before either commits; added `reserveAIQuota()`/`settleAIQuotaReservation()`/`cancelAIQuotaReservation()` (`lib/plan-limits.ts`) wrapping `checkAIQuota` with a hold-first/check-second sequence (passing `estimatedTokens=0` to `checkAIQuota` since the hold already carries the estimate, avoiding double-counting); fixed the `usageWithPending` fast-pool gap to always merge pending tokens using a default weight of 1 when no `modelId` is given. Wired the new reserve/settle/cancel triplet into all 5 routes (`complete`, `inline-edit`, `inline-completion`, `action`, `query`) on every exit path (model-access denial, demo/no-provider, provider success, and the outer catch). **Tests:** new `__tests__/production/law-xvi-p2f1-chat-cost-guard-audit.test.ts` — proves scenario (a) with `decideSpendLane` (free+no-BYOK+exhausted → `blocked`; BYOK → own-pay lane, not a bypass) and reproduces scenario (b)'s race pre-fix (`checkAIQuota` alone lets two 800-token concurrent calls both pass against a 1000-token remaining budget) then proves it closed post-fix (`reserveAIQuota` denies the second concurrent call; cancel releases the hold with zero charge). Full `npx vitest run __tests__/production` sweep: **541/543 passed** (up from 536/538 baseline; +5 new tests, 0 regressions — remaining 2 failures are the pre-existing unrelated `focus1-focus2-spend-l5.test.ts` renderer-honesty gap, confirmed unrelated by diffing against a stash of these exact changes). `npx eslint` on all 7 touched files: **zero new errors** (pre-existing unused-var/no-undef/no-explicit-any findings in `action/route.ts` and `financial-ledger.ts` confirmed pre-existing via git-stash diff, not introduced by this change). `npm run typecheck` still blocked (`node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc` missing, not touched per standing instruction); manually verified via hoisted `npx -p typescript tsc --noEmit` that the 7 touched files produce **zero** errors (pre-existing unrelated errors remain elsewhere in the monorepo, e.g. `@react-three/fiber` JSX intrinsics). **Honest scope note:** this round closes the concrete TOCTOU security/cost hole and documents the two-system split; it does **not** perform a full architectural unification of chat/complete billing onto `CreativeCostGuard`'s byok/usage_bucket/wallet pool-funding primitives or onto spend-resolver's dual Fast/Premium pool math — that is a larger cross-cutting billing-unification project, explicitly deferred to a future round, not attempted piecemeal here. |
| 2026-08-08 p2f-2 | **P2f item #2 / P2b BLOCKER 1-2 DONE — `usd-gltf-exporter.ts` `success:true` + empty artifact confirmed real and fixed at the root.** Investigated `cloud-web-app/web/lib/production/usd-gltf-exporter.ts` (the exact file named in the P2b sweep) and cross-checked against `usd-integrator.ts` (real, tested, already honest per commit `868fe37ce`'s `USD_BROWSER_VIEWER_SHIP_STATUS='HELD'` revert). **Verdict: real bug, not a false-positive** — `UniversalSceneManager` is a second, separate module (`git log` shows a single commit `93046e4d7`, zero other commits, zero importers anywhere in the repo besides itself) exporting a legacy stub class never wired into any route/dispatcher. `importSemanticAsset` returned `success: true` with a **fabricated** `semantics` object (hardcoded `category:'vehicle', confidence:0.98`) for a buffer it never actually parsed (`// TODO: Implementar parser binário WASM`), plus a literally empty `entityPayload: {}` — a direct Law XVI Zero-MVP violation. `exportToIndustryStandard` resolved a bare `new ArrayBuffer(0)` with **no discriminant field at all** (not even a `success` flag), so a caller could not even detect the empty payload as a failure. Both methods also used raw `console.log` (violates the `no-console` lint gate / `createComponentLogger` rule). **Root cause:** the WASM binary semantic parser and the ECS-linear→node-hierarchy serializer were never implemented; the stub fabricated a success response instead of failing closed. **Fix (fail-closed, no fake payload fabricated):** rewrote both methods to return a discriminated `{success:true,...}` / `{success:false, blockedReason:'not_implemented'\|'empty_payload', message}` result; `importSemanticAsset` now refuses to report success for an unparsed buffer (distinct `empty_payload` reason for a literally-empty input) instead of inventing a category/physics guess; `exportToIndustryStandard` now returns a `not_implemented`/HELD result instead of a bare empty buffer with zero signal. Replaced both `console.log` calls with `createComponentLogger('usd-gltf-exporter')` warnings. **Tests:** new `__tests__/production/usd-gltf-exporter-honesty.test.ts` (5 tests) proves neither method can return `success:true` for an unparsed/empty payload, that empty-buffer input gets its own reason code, and adds a regression guard asserting any future `success:true` result must carry a non-empty `entityPayload`/`buffer`. `npx vitest run __tests__/production/usd-gltf-exporter-honesty.test.ts __tests__/production/ai-v1e-j5-j7-core.test.ts` — **16/16 PASS**. Added a `pagehide`/`beforeunload`/`visibilitychange`(hidden) safety net that force-flushes any still-queued entries synchronously — closes the most common loss window (tab close, link navigation, mobile backgrounding) without touching the async multi-tab LWW path. `npx vitest run __tests__/storage/ui-persistence-spine.test.ts __tests__/storage/ui-persistence-critical-inventory.test.ts` **20/20 PASS** after the change. **Logged, not fixed this pass (P1, real but requires more than a small safe edit):** `Outliner3D.tsx` is **600 LoC** and `PropertiesPanel3D.tsx` is **701 LoC**, both over the binding 500-LoC limit (100/201 lines over) — splitting them safely needs `typecheck` (currently blocked, see below) to verify no import breakage, so deferred rather than risking an unverified split. | 2026-07-27 | **Onda G resume, item 1/7: World Partition desktop wire + real hydrate/evict bug fix (letter ip4)** — Founder-directed resume of the paused Onda G "wire real math into the frame/render graph" list (7 items: micro-poly decimation, GI/radiance cascades, VSM, **world partition** ✅ this round, USD export, anisotropic BRDF, MaterialX/OpenVDB doc pointers). `svo_terrain_world_partition.rs` had **zero** IPC surface (no `kernel_*_wire` file existed at all — not even the static-probe pattern every sibling module has). **Root-cause bug found and fixed in the kernel module itself, not just wired as-is:** `update_stream()` measured `pages_hydrated`/`pages_evicted` by comparing the `active` boolean at a fixed ring-buffer slot index across ticks; since the grid is recentred on the camera's current chunk every tick with the same relative `dx,dz` offsets, the active/culled pattern at each slot is nearly camera-position-invariant — so the churn counters were reporting ~zero on real camera movement almost always, i.e. the metric looked wired but was not actually measuring streaming behaviour. Fixed to compare **chunk identity** (`chunk_x`/`chunk_z`) at each slot: only a genuine identity change counts as hydrate/evict. Added `run_svo_terrain_world_partition_soak()` (5-tick soak: stationary→zero churn, long jump→real hydrate+evict, stationary again→settles, independent same-seed replay matches tick 1, LOD bands monotonic by distance, NaN camera pose safely rejected) + `probe_svo_terrain_world_partition_reports_ready`/soak/zero-alloc-slot-mutation tests, **5 new kernel tests, 885/885 total green** (`cd packages/aethel-kernel-rust && cargo test --lib -- --test-threads=1`). New wire `apps/studio-local/src-tauri/src/kernel_svo_terrain_world_partition_wire.rs`: standard `probe_svo_terrain_world_partition_cmd` / `run_kernel_svo_terrain_world_partition_soak_cmd` **plus** — unlike every sibling `kernel_*_wire` (fresh kernel + fixed fixture recomputed from scratch per call) — a genuinely **stateful, per-frame** `world_partition_stream_tick_cmd(camera_pos)` backed by Tauri-managed `Mutex<WorldPartitionStreamState>` that persists the kernel + ring buffer across calls, plus `world_partition_stream_reset_cmd`. Registered in `lib.rs`/`main.rs` (`.manage(Mutex::new(WorldPartitionStreamState::default()))` + 4 new `generate_handler!` entries). **Honest scope — HELD, not claimed:** the live desktop viewport does **not** yet call `world_partition_stream_tick_cmd` from a real per-frame render loop this round (no `wgpu_renderer.rs` GPU-side chunk mesh streaming/culling wired to the resulting chunk set); the IPC command + persistent camera-driven state is real end-to-end, the frontend caller and GPU-visible effect are not. `svoTerrainWorldPartitionUe5ParityReady` stays **false** (no HLOD/Nanite-equivalent/50km²/no-loading-screen claim). This native Rust path is explicitly distinct from — and does not replace — the already-shipped TS `lib/world-streaming/partition-streaming.ts` web/R3F path (letters cg/ck), which remains the actual shipped web product surface. **Bonus fixes touched while verifying the crate-wide gate (pre-existing, from the prior Wave G.3 commit, not from this item):** removed a real `unused_mut` in `entropy_gpu_particles.rs` (the exact one flagged in the prior session's own notes as still outstanding), 2 `clippy::manual_clamp` → `.clamp(...)`, and one documented `#[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]` on `run_steps` (9 independent physical-simulation params; same trade-off already justified crate-wide in `aethel-kernel-rust/src/lib.rs`) so the **full** `apps/studio-local/src-tauri` crate (not just `--lib`) passes `cargo check` and `cargo clippy -- -D warnings` clean (only the one pre-existing, architecture-conditional `desktop_soak.rs:743` warning remains — it only fires on non-x86 hosts; this container is aarch64, the project's real target is x86_64, the `mut` is genuinely required there). **Environment note (not a code issue):** ran in a fresh aarch64 Linux container (no GPU, no `E:\aethel-target-gnu`) — `cargo check`/`clippy`/kernel `cargo test --lib` all completed and are green; the full `apps/studio-local` **binary-target** `cargo test --lib` (which also builds the huge `wgpu`+`wasmtime`+`tauri` dependency tree in test-harness mode) did not finish linking in this container (resource-constrained, not a compile error — `cargo check` on the identical code already passed clean) and was not re-attempted after the second timeout; no code in this item carries its own bin-target unit tests (matches the established sibling-wire convention — the tested logic lives entirely in the kernel crate, already 885/885 green).
